15: * A Voidcraft component cover: a compact part mounted on a face of a Voidcraft hull block.
16: *
17: * <p>
18: * Renders the cover part's own 16×16 icon as a full-face texture over the hull (pass 24: each cover has a
19: * dedicated icon — the old mirrored-block icon shared the framebox/planet-sheet art). Carries its
20: * {@link VoidcraftCoverComponent} so the assembler scan and the stat math can see it (covers count toward the ship's
21: * stats, and thruster covers count toward thrust only on the ship's REAR face, the assembler side — pass 24 flip).
32: Cover instances are constructed at mount time — long after the icon phase — so the texture must come
33: from the load-phase cache (VoidcraftTextures), never re-resolved by name here.
37: * Full-face texture so the mounted part replaces the face art.
43: * Fallback overlay (same icon) if a renderer only draws overlays.
54: * @return the cover part behind this cover instance (null only if the item was unregistered at build time)
==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\gui\VoidcraftProgramGui.java ====
43: * The Voidcraft programming GUI (pass 33 UI, UI-2) — a linear block-based visual programming view
44: * (Scratch-style), opened by right-clicking the controller block, or by right-clicking a digitized blueprint
45: * item (ship / base) in hand.
46: *
47: * <p>
48: * LEFT: the current program as horizontal blocks (loops indented one level per depth), each block showing its
49: * arguments as visually distinct SLOTS; a CURSOR (the selected row + slot) drives the slot editor above the list
50: * (text field + Set / VAR); each row has ▲ / ▼ (reorder) and ✕ (delete) buttons; the footer shows the program
51: * caps and the last server rejection.
52: *
53: * <p>
54: * RIGHT: (1) USS VALUES — pick a global USS variable slot (0..255) and assign it as a reference into the
55: * selected slot (SLOT SELECTION ONLY — no live USS value display, user spec UI-2); (2) ARGUMENTS — helpers that
56: * fill the selected MOVE target (Nearest planet / Random planet / Random ripple), shown only when the craft can
57: * MOVE; (3) COMMANDS — the palette with an [Add] per command (inserts at the cursor), gated by the craft's
58: * CAPABILITY SET (a ship without thrusters gets no MOVE row; without mining power no MINE row, etc.) and each
59: * row's tooltip carries the command description + the craft's stat line for it (speed / mining power / scan
60: * power / siphon power / construction power); (4) PRESETS — Miner / Starlifter / Explorer / Build / Clear, each
61: * hidden when the craft lacks the capability the preset needs.
62: *
63: * <p>
64: * Sync (server authoritative): the program lives in the {@link VoidcraftProgramSource} (the controller block
65: * NBT or the blueprint item NBT); the ROWS sync (S2C list of row JSON) drives the left list; every edit is one
66: * C2S action ({@code uss.action} = one action JSON, see {@link tectech.voidcraft.uss.USSProgramSync}) — the
67: * server runs the editor and pushes the new rows + note.
71: command palette node specs (see USSProgramSync#readNode)
88: the craft's capability set (the capability system): which commands the underlying ship / base can run —
89: read once at build (the caps never change while the GUI is open)
92: sync handlers (created in build)
97: client-side CURSOR (selected row + slot)
103: input fields (client-side text; the StringValue binding is the source of truth for the text)
109: selection highlight (UI-3): per-row button references, rebuilt with the list
115: highlight / slot-kind backgrounds (UI-3)
143: unreadable payload — ignore the action
163: region left panel (program block list + cursor + footer)
166: The DynamicLinkedSyncHandler is bound to the rows sync value: it rebuilds the list
167: automatically whenever the rows change (initial S2C push + every server update),
168: and pushes the initial content when the widget is initialised.
185: * The CURSOR line: the selected row + slot (with its current value), then the text field + Set / VAR.
187: A bound value is required for the text field to render (bare text fields drop out of the flow)
272: re-apply the selection to the freshly built rows (or clear it if the row is gone)
279: * One block row: indent, label, argument slots, up / down / delete.
368: * A short per-block description for the label tooltip (UI-3).
412: endregion
414: region right panel (USS values / arguments / commands / presets)
420: The capability system: the palette only offers the commands the craft can run (a ship without
421: thruster covers gets no MOVE row; the presets needing a missing capability are hidden). WRITE /
422: READ / WAIT / STOP and the flow commands are always available (they need no ship capability).
440: the ARGUMENTS helpers fill the MOVE target — only useful when MOVE is available
522: * A palette row: label + [Add] (inserts the node at the cursor). The tooltip carries the description plus an
523: * optional STAT LINE (the craft's stats for the command — the ship's speed for MOVE, its mining power for
524: * MINE, and so on; flow rows carry none).
548: * A COMMAND-palette row: the command's label + [Add], with the craft's STAT LINE for the command in the
549: * tooltip (the capability system: the ship's speed for MOVE, its mining power for MINE, and so on).
555: * A FLOW-block row (IF / WHILE / REPEAT — always available, no stat line).
560: * An ARGUMENT helper: fills the selected MOVE target slot with the given target.
582: endregion
584: region cursor + actions
619: * Restore every row button to its default look (theme background / slot-kind background).
640: * (Re)apply the selection highlight to the currently built rows; clears the selection if the row is gone.
655: the selected row no longer exists (it was deleted) — drop the selection
676: * [Set]: write the text field as a literal into the selected slot (param / count / condition side).
702: target / index / value / slot / from / to / ticks
706: * [VAR] / [Assign]: assign the selected USS slot as a reference (WRITE value / condition side).
727: any other slot kind: a USS reference is not valid there — silent no-op
730: * ARGUMENT helper: set the selected MOVE target slot.
738: * Op slot: cycle EQ → NEQ → LT → GT → EQ.
753: endregion
755: region action JSON (one C2S action = one edit)
762: payload too large / bad — the action is dropped
823: "op" is the action discriminator — the operator goes under "operator"
849: * INSERT at the cursor: before the selected row (its list, at its index) or at the end of the program.

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\gui\VoidcraftProgramItemSource.java ====
22: * {@link VoidcraftProgramSource} for a digitized blueprint item (ship / base) in the player inventory: the
23: * program is loaded from the item NBT at open; every accepted edit is applied on the server and written back
24: * to the item in its inventory slot (the slot stack is replaced and the updated slot pushed to the client).
25: *
26: * <p>
27: * The capability set is DERIVED from the item itself (the capability system): a digitized SHIP item carries its
28: * exact denormalized stats (speed / mining / scan / siphon / construction power), so the editor offers exactly
29: * the commands that ship can run; a base item offers MINE (mining power) + REPAIR (a repair bay in the
30: * blueprint).
46: * @param data the GUI data (carries the player and the slot index of the blueprint item)
47: * @param item the blueprint item instance (ship / base) — used to verify the slot still holds it
56: The stat lines (tooltips) are derived from the same source as the caps — read once here (the item's
57: stats never change while the GUI is open).
82: * The capability set of the blueprint item (ship stats / base mining power + repair bays).
102: a ship never repairs (a REPAIR on a ship SKIPs)
130: server authoritative — actions only ever run here
170: * Write the edited program back into the blueprint item in its inventory slot. Skipped when the item is no
171: * longer in the slot (the GUI was left open while the item was dropped / moved).
==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\gui\VoidcraftProgramSource.java ====
9: * Where the programming GUI (see {@link VoidcraftProgramGui}) reads the program from and sends its edits to.
10: *
11: * <p>
12: * Two sources exist: the in-world CONTROLLER component (program in block NBT) and the digitized blueprint ITEM
13: * (ship / base) in the player inventory (program in item NBT, see {@link VoidcraftProgramItemSource}).
14: *
15: * <p>
16: * The source also reports the craft's CAPABILITY SET (the capability system) + a per-command STAT LINE (the
17: * command tooltip): the GUI only offers the commands the craft can run, and every command row shows what the
18: * craft actually does with it (its speed / mining power / scan power / siphon power / construction power).
23: * The program as flat row wire strings for the GUI list sync (see
24: * {@link tectech.voidcraft.uss.USSProgramView#rowsJsonList}).
28: * The last server-side rejection reason ("" when none).
32: * Apply one GUI action JSON (see {@link tectech.voidcraft.uss.USSProgramSync}). Runs on the server; must
33: * NEVER throw (bad action → rejection → the note is set).
38: * The craft's COMMAND CAPABILITY SET (the capability system): which program commands the underlying ship /
39: * base can actually run (the GUI hides the rest and the server rejects edits outside it).
44: * The per-command STAT LINE for a command row's tooltip ("Speed: 3.2", "Mining power: 120", …; "" when the
45: * command has no stat or the craft has none of it). Indexed by command id (0..{@link USSCommand#MAX_ID}).
==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\item\ItemUSSController.java ====
24: * USS Controller (EoH rework, Phase 2 vertical slice; Phase 4 pass 1 — star types).
25: *
26: * <p>
27: * The ignition source for the Unstable Solar System multiblock. Insert one into the system's controller slot
28: * (sneak-right-click on the casing) to ignite the star. The controller is <em>consumed</em> when the star burns
29: * out — one controller = one star life.
30: *
31: * <p>
32: * Phase 4 pass 1: three variants, one per star class — the <strong>item decides the star type</strong> (it is
33: * no longer derived from the spacetime tier; the tier still scales the star's rendering and the miner ore band):
34: * <ul>
35: * <li>meta 0 — Main Sequence (original placeholder icon)</li>
36: * <li>meta 1 — White Dwarf (cold-white placeholder icon)</li>
37: * <li>meta 2 — Supermassive (hot-gold placeholder icon)</li>
38: * </ul>
39: *
40: * @see tectech.voidcraft.uss.MTEUnstableSolarSystem
41: * @see docs/Voidcraft_Implementation_Plan.md
47: * Number of star-class variants (meta values 0–2).
55: Item name keys: item.tt.ussController.name (meta 0), item.tt.ussController.1.name (meta 1),
56: item.tt.ussController.2.name (meta 2) — 1.7.10 appends the meta to the unlocalized name (see
57: ItemVoidcraftCovers.getUnlocalizedName for the same pattern).
64: * @param meta 0–2
65: * @return the star class selected by that controller variant (null for unknown metas).
76: * @param stack a controller stack (may be null or any other item)
77: * @return the star class that stack would ignite, or null when it is not a recognized controller (the machine
78: *         then leaves the star cold — the slot keeps the unrecognized item).
88: * @param starType the star class to ignite with
89: * @return a single controller of that class (null for unknown types).

40: * Voidcraft loader (EoH rework, Phase 0+1+1.5+2).
41: *
42: * <p>

48: * </ul>
49: *
50: * <p>
51: * PASS 23 (user spec): covers are the PRIMARY components — all ship functionality comes from them. The only
52: * placeable full blocks are the Voidcraft Controller and the Voidcraft Frame (renamed Utility Block, a
53: * mostly-transparent framebox). Full blocks live on the standard GT machine block (wrench-facing, per-face
54: * texture slots, six cover slots); the assembler scan picks blocks + covers up.
55: *
56: * <p>

58: * ignore recipes until explicitly asked).

60: * <p>

62: * {@link VoidcraftConfig}).
63: *
64: * <p>

71: * in the load phase; the block self-registers with Forge in its constructor.

79: * indexed by component meta.

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\machine\MTEVoidbaseAssembler.java ====
62: * Voidbase Assembler.
63: *
64: * <p>
65: * A 15×15×3 multiblock whose front face (15×15) looks out over a 15×15×15 build volume. It scans that volume,
66: * validates the placed components with the BASE rules (the ship rules minus the thruster audit — a Voidbase is an
67: * immobile station) — exactly one controller, a frame, component/cover tier ≤ the assembler circuit tier — then
68: * digitizes the build into a single non-stackable, REUSABLE {@code ItemVoidbaseBlueprint} and clears the component
69: * blocks. Constructor voidcrafts carry the blueprint data and a parts loadout when they are launched from the
70: * gateway; several constructors can build the same base (the first one creates the construction site, the rest
71: * fill it).
78: * Scan volume: 15 wide × 15 tall × 15 deep in front of the machine's front face (the base volume).
84: * 15 wide × 15 tall × 3 deep. Controller at the front-center (x=7, y=7, z=0). Hatch slots (C) ring the front
85: * face, 'B' (TT casing meta 7) pads the front interior, 'A' (TT casing meta 4) fills the two back planes.
101: * The 15×15×3 shape: hatch ring on the front face, controller at the front-center, padded interior.
123: Digitized-but-not-yet-output base (persisted across chunk reloads)
128: * The controller's stored program captured from the scanned build volume — written into the digitized
129: * blueprint item's NBT ({@link VoidcraftNbt#TAG_PROGRAM}) at output. Null = the controller had no program.
134: * The multiblock component controllers the last scan found in the volume — the scan audit forces each one's own
135: * structure check, then applies the component's stats only to the formed structures.
163: * Highest component tier allowed by the integrated circuits in the input buses.
164: * No circuit → tier 0 (base components only).
180: * World position of the scan cell at (i, j, depth): i/j in [-7, 7] across the front face, depth in [1, 15]
181: * along the facing direction.
186: Two perpendicular axes spanning the front face
189: facing up/down: the build plane is horizontal (X-Z)
193: facing east/west: the build plane is vertical (Y-Z)
197: facing north/south: the build plane is vertical (X-Y)
207: * Scan the 15×15×15 volume in front of the machine's front face.
208: *
209: * <p>
210: * Captures each component block (and its facing) plus every Voidcraft cover mounted on any of its six faces —
211: * covers are part of the station and contribute their stats. Multiblock component blocks (their own GT
212: * multiblocks) are captured too; each multiblock controller found in the volume is collected for the scan
213: * audit, which applies the component's stats only when the component's own structure is formed.
214: *
215: * @return the scanned blueprint, or null if the volume contains something that is neither air nor a Voidcraft
216: *         component block
234: out of world counts as empty
248: foreign block in the volume
268: store the cover's side in GRID space (the blueprint's depth axis is the
269: assembler's front, not a world axis), so the same world direction maps to the
270: same grid side no matter which way the assembler itself faces.
279: A multiblock component controller — its stats only count when its own structure is
280: formed (the audit in checkProcessing_EM forces the structure check).
300: * Clear the component blocks of a digitized base from the world (only cells that still hold a Voidcraft
301: * component MTE — classic full blocks or multiblock component blocks — so foreign blocks placed meanwhile are
302: * never destroyed).
333: The multiblock components found in the volume: force each controller's own structure check, then audit
334: (unformed structure / structure reaching beyond the volume → the scan fails with that error key).
366: The controller's program rides the item NBT (vc_program) into the base payload at launch.
379: ---- client visuals (scan wireframe / scanning planes / preview hologram — see RenderVoidcraftAssembler)

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\machine\MTEVoidcraftAssembler.java ====
62: * Voidcraft Assembler (EoH rework, Phase 1).
63: *
64: * <p>
65: * A 5×5×3 multiblock whose front face (5×5) looks out over a 5×5×10 build volume. It scans that volume, validates
66: * the placed components — full blocks plus any covers mounted on their faces (exactly one controller, at least one
67: * engine, component/cover tier ≤ the assembler circuit tier, net thrust not fully cancelled) — then digitizes the
68: * ship into a single non-stackable {@code ItemVoidcraft} and clears the component blocks.

67: * Voidcraft Gateway (EoH rework, Phase 3).
68: *
69: * <p>
70: * A 3×3×3 BA0-cased shell with the controller at the front-face center, ringed by the hatch-capable perimeter.
71: * The INPUT buses are the ship source: feed a digitized
72: * {@link ItemVoidcraft} through them and — when a valid Unstable Solar System (star ignited) and a Storage Bay
73: * are both within range — the gateway launches the ship
74: * on a mining mission: the ship flies out to the star, mines, and returns with cargo, which the USS delivers to
75: * the nearest bay. A ship's integrity is its time limit (it drops 1 per second while in the USS): a ship that
76: * finishes before it expires is re-emitted into the gateway's OUTPUT bus (integrity back at maximum); one that
77: * hits 0 is lost with its cargo.
78: *
79: * <p>
80: * No energy hatches, no recipes — the interaction surface is the BLUEPRINT slot (right-click with a Voidbase
81: * blueprint item in hand — the blueprint is KEPT, the constructor ship carries a data copy) plus the front-face
82: * hatch ring: the input buses feed the ship + the Constructor's parts loadout (component blocks + covers as
83: * items) — MINER / STARLIFTER ships launch with just the ship item, no parts. The output bus is the return path
84: * for the surviving ship and a Constructor's unused parts — the structure check requires an input bus AND an
85: * output bus.
86: *
87: * <p>
88: * The gateway renders nothing itself: a ship's hologram exists only while it is in flight, and that in-flight anchor
89: * is owned by the Unstable Solar System (see {@link MTEUnstableSolarSystem}).
96: * How far (blocks) the gateway scans for a USS / Storage Bay.
100: * Minimum interval (ticks) between target scans while a ship waits on the input side. The scan sweeps the
101: * whole sphere at full resolution, so the result is cached instead of re-scanning every tick.
106: * 3×3×3 shell: the <strong>front face</strong> (z=0) is the controller (center) ringed by the hatch-capable
107: * perimeter 'C' (input buses + input hatches — the SHIP source, a launch consumes one Voidcraft from them,
108: * plus the Constructor's parts loadout; and the output bus — the return path for the surviving ship and a
109: * Constructor's unused parts on mission complete; non-hatch ring
110: * cells render as HighPowerCasing, same family convention as the Storage Bay and the Energy Infuser), the two
111: * back planes are BA0 casing meta 10. The controller sits at the front-face center (x=1, y=1, z=0 — the same
112: * convention as the Storage Bay and the Assembler; a buried center controller would be unreachable once the
113: * shell is closed).
124: InputBus: the Constructor parts path (component blocks + covers, incl. ME item buses).
125: InputHatch slot 0 is also read for items (the standard GT hatch slot).
126: OutputBus: the return path for a Constructor's unused parts (mission complete).
127: Non-hatch ring cells fall back to the HighPowerCasing render.
134: * Rate-limits launch-failure chat (ticks).
138: * Cached launch targets, refreshed at most every {@link #SCAN_COOLDOWN_TICKS} (see {@link #refreshLaunchTargets}).
145: * The Voidbase blueprint in the blueprint slot (a single {@code ItemVoidbaseBlueprint} stack — REUSABLE: it
146: * stays here between launches; each Constructor launch copies its data + a parts loadout into the ship
147: * payload). Server-only: the gateway has no GUI and renders nothing of its own, so no client sync.
151: * Scan detail buffer (per-sweep diagnostics for failed target searches).
154: * Rate-limits scan-diagnostic chat (ticks).
158: * Plain log4j logger at INFO (NOT {@code GTLog.conditionalLogger}, which forces Level.OFF) — gateway lifecycle
159: * and scan diagnostics must actually reach the game log.
181: The input bus is the ship source (and the Constructor parts path); the output bus is the return path
182: (surviving ships + unused Constructor parts) — both are required.

196: blueprint is KEPT in the slot for the next launch). Ships are not inserted by hand — the gateway
197: consumes them from its input buses.
211: * Persist the blueprint slot (ships live in the input buses, nothing of the machine's own to save).
250: region launch
253: * Try to launch a ship from the input side (input hatch slot 0 + input buses). The ship item is consumed
254: * from the input ONLY when the launch succeeds — a rejected launch leaves the ship in the input (and the
255: * error is reported, rate-limited, to nearby players).
265: idle — no ship on the input side
268: The ship payload is the item's tag compound (the vc_* keys sit at its top level — see
269: ItemVoidcraft.getBlueprint). The full ItemStack NBT nests it one level deeper under the vanilla
270: "tag" key, so writeToNBT must NOT be used here. It is COPIED before the mission is loaded: the
271: Constructor path mutates the payload (vc_build_mission + vc_build_loadout), and the input item is
272: consumed by full-NBT equality against the bus's stack — a mutated payload would never match.
281: MINER / STARLIFTER (mining cargo), EXPLORER (spacetime-ripple scanning) or CONSTRUCTOR
282: (Voidbase construction) may launch; a ship with none of those roles cannot be sent out.
303: A CONSTRUCTOR needs the blueprint in the blueprint slot: the ship leaves loaded with a DATA COPY of
304: the blueprint (the item stays in the slot — reusable) plus a parts loadout that FILLS its cargo from
305: the input buses (capped at the ship's cargo space; the CONSTRUCT leg credits the site with what it
306: needs, the first Constructor creates the construction site, the rest fill it). A CONSTRUCTOR+MINER/
307: STARLIFTER hybrid without a blueprint falls back to a regular mining mission; a pure CONSTRUCTOR
308: without one is rejected (the ship stays in the input, no wasted flights).
326: The launch is in flight — consume the ship from the input side. The pull matches on item +
327: payload NBT (the consumed ship IS the found ship, even when several ships wait on the input),
328: and the find + pull run in the same tick, so the found stack cannot vanish in between.
340: * Load a Voidbase construction mission into the ship payload: copy the blueprint's data (the blueprint item
341: * stays in the slot — reusable) and FILL the ship's cargo with parts from the input buses — for each part of
342: * the parts list (blueprint order), as many as the buses hold, until the ship's cargo space (the hold
343: * capacity, in cargo units) is full or the buses run dry. The CONSTRUCT leg credits the site with what it
344: * needs; whatever is carried beyond the site's needs is discarded there (the site's existing rule). A zero
345: * fill is fine — the first Constructor creates the construction site even without parts, the rest fill it.
346: *

348: *         the blueprint slot (no error is reported — the caller decides: a hybrid role may still launch as a
349: *         regular mission)

365: Fill the ship's cargo from the input buses: for each part of the parts list (blueprint order), as
366: many as the buses hold, until the cargo space (the hold capacity, in cargo units) is full.
401: * The construction anchor implied by the blueprint's stored program: the first MOVE instruction's target,
402: * when it resolves to a STATIC anchor (STAR, a specific PLANET index, a specific RIPPLE index). Dynamic
403: * targets (nearest/random planet, unscanned ripple) and non-anchor targets (HOME, SHIP) cannot be resolved
404: * here — null (the launch log reports the anchor as dynamic).

437: * placeable blocks + the multiblock components — see {@link VoidcraftLoader#blockItem}), {@code cover.<NAME>}
438: * → the cover item (meta = cover id).

463: * parts-list key format — the CONSTRUCT handler credits the site part by part).
483: * Sum the matching dust stacks over the slots the depletion can reach: input-hatch slot 0 of every input hatch
484: * plus every slot of every input bus (the same slot set {@code depleteInput} walks).
517: * Deplete {@code amount} of the matching dust from the input slots (hatch slot 0 first, then bus slots — partial
518: * per-slot takes, so split stacks drain completely; returns the amount actually taken).
561: * The first Voidcraft on the input side (input-hatch slot 0 of every input hatch, then every slot of every
562: * input bus — the same slot set the Constructor loadout pulls from). A copy is returned (the bus's stack
563: * is not touched); null when the input side holds no ship (the gateway idles — no error).
593: * Consume one ship from the input side — the stack equal to {@code match} (item + payload NBT, so the
594: * consumed ship IS the found ship even when several ships wait on the input side). Called only on a
595: * successful launch, in the same tick as the find.
596: *
597: * @param match the ship found by {@link #findShipInput()}
598: * @return true when a ship was consumed
629: * Output an item stack into the output bus buffers — each bus absorbs as much as it can, and whatever does
630: * not fit stays in the given stack (its {@code stackSize} is reduced by the absorbed amount). Used for the
631: * returned ship and for a Constructor's unused parts.
632: *
633: * @param stack the stack to output (mutated in place: only the absorbed amount is removed)
634: * @return the amount actually absorbed by the output buses (0 when the gateway has none)
651: * (Re-)scan for the nearest ignited USS and a valid Storage Bay within {@link #SCAN_RADIUS}, at most once every
652: * {@link #SCAN_COOLDOWN_TICKS} — the sweep checks every position in the sphere (full resolution), which is too
653: * expensive to repeat every tick while a ship waits on the input side. Between sweeps the cached result
654: * (including "not found") is reused.
657: NB: the `!= Long.MIN_VALUE` checks are NOT optional — the sentinels start at Long.MIN_VALUE, so
658: `aTick - Long.MIN_VALUE` overflows to a large negative and `< SCAN_COOLDOWN_TICKS` is always true,
659: which would early-return on every call and the scan (and the diagnostics below) would never run.
660: Short-circuiting on the sentinel guarantees the first call always scans.
666: A FULL USS (all ship slots occupied) is NOT a valid launch target: it would reject the launch
667: (uss_busy) — and a Constructor launch is rejected only AFTER its loadout was already pulled from the
668: inputs (material loss). A USS with free slots is a valid target even while other ships are in flight
669: (Phase 4 pass 4 — multiple ships per star system).

678: the system view (the fleet anchor's gateway list, independent of the fleet's ship list). The client-side
679: MTE mirror runs the same scan, but its registration is a no-op there (the fleet anchor sync is
680: server-only).

728: * (null when none is found).

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\machine\MTEVoidcraftStorageBay.java ====
51: * Voidcraft Storage Bay (EoH rework, Phase 3).
52: *
53: * <p>
54: * A 5×5×3 multiblock holding the shared {@link VoidcraftCargoPool} (16 slots) that mining ships deliver into and
55: * that players pull out of.
56: *
57: * <p>
58: * I/O model (no energy, no maintenance, no recipes — this is a buffer):
59: * <ul>
60: * <li>input buses push their contents into the pool (merge-aware),</li>
61: * <li>the pool pushes out as much as fits into the output busses (void-protection ON — leftovers stay in the
62: * pool),</li>
63: * <li>ships deliver cargo straight through the output busses first (an ME output bus is the bulk sink: it takes any
64: * number of items into its cache and flushes them to the ME network),</li>
65: * <li>Phase 4 pass 1: Starlifter missions also deliver <em>fluid</em> cargo (Stellar Plasma) into a parallel
66: * {@link VoidcraftFluidPool}, pumped out through output <em>hatches</em> (an ME output hatch is the bulk sink) —
67: * same ME-first / pool-fallback / never-silently-void contract as the item path,</li>
68: * <li>both pump directions run every machine tick while the structure is valid.</li>
69: * </ul>
70: *
71: * <p>
72: * Casing: BA0 meta 10 (back planes) / meta 11 (front face ring), buses around the front face — the same 5×5×3
73: * silhouette as the Voidcraft Assembler.
74: *
75: * <p>
76: * The pool persists in NBT across chunk reloads and collapses (it belongs to the bay, not to the USS).
86: * 5 wide × 5 tall × 3 deep. Controller at the front-center (x=2, y=2, z=0). Bus slots (C) ring the front face,
87: * 'A' (BA0 casing meta 10) fills the two back planes, 'B' (BA0 casing meta 11) pads the front ring.
101: Input/OutputBus: the item path (buses, incl. ME item buses). OutputHatch: the Phase 4 pass 1
102: FLUID path — output hatches (incl. ME output hatches) accept the pool's Stellar Plasma.
109: * The shared cargo pool (16 slots).
112: * The shared fluid pool (Phase 4 pass 1: Starlifter Stellar Plasma / dwarf-matter fluids).
139: Not a recipe machine — the buffer logic runs in onPostTick.
152: 1) Input buses → pool (merge-aware; the remainder stays in the bus).
161: Deplete exactly what was inserted (depleteInput removes a matching stack from the buses).
167: 2) Pool → output busses (as much as fits). Void protection is ON so the leftover stays in the pool slot —
168: the old addOutputPartial() (protection OFF for this machine) silently voided whatever the busses
169: (incl. a full ME cache) could not take, while the pool slot was drained completely.
183: accepted out; the remainder stays in toEject
191: 3) Fluid pool → output hatches (Phase 4 pass 1). Same contract: void protection ON → the accepted mB
192: comes back, the remainder stays in the pool slot (ME output hatches are the bulk sink for it).
200: unresolvable (mod removed) — the entry sits until it is pulled
204: accepted mB out; the remainder stays in toEject
214: * Deliver an abstract cargo (called by the USS when a ship completes a mining leg).
215: *
216: * <p>
217: * <strong>This is the delivery boundary</strong>: the cargo arrives as abstract {@code {id, Damage, amount}}
218: * entries (see {@link USSShipCargo}) and is converted to 64-chunked item stacks here. Delivery order:
219: * <ol>
220: * <li><strong>Output busses first</strong> — via GT's ejection helper with void protection ON, so anything the
221: * busses cannot take is <em>not</em> lost. An ME output bus is the intended bulk sink: it accepts any number of
222: * items into its cache (default 1600, extendable with a storage cell or void cell) and flushes them to the ME
223: * network every 40 ticks, honoring the storage cell's partition filter. Regular output busses take what their
224: * slots hold.</li>
225: * <li><strong>The 16-slot pool</strong> for whatever the busses could not take (buffer for a later pump-out).</li>
226: * <li><strong>Item entities at the bay</strong> as the last resort — never silently lost.</li>
227: * </ol>
228: *
229: * @param items abstract cargo entry list ({@code USSShipCargo.TAG_ITEMS} format)
230: * @return true if everything was accepted (no drop), false if some was dropped at the door
239: 1) Output busses (ME bus = bulk sink; regular bus = slot capacity). Void protection ON → the accepted
240: count comes back, the remainder stays in the stack.
248: 2) Whatever the busses did not take → the pool.
254: insert() subtracts what it accepted; a remainder means the pool overflowed.
260: 3) Last resort: drop at the bay (never silently lost).
276: * Deliver an abstract FLUID cargo (called by the USS when a Starlifter completes its mining leg — Phase 4
277: * pass 1).
278: *
279: * <p>
280: * <strong>This is the fluid delivery boundary</strong>: the cargo arrives as abstract
281: * {@code {material, mB}} entries ({@code USSShipCargo.TAG_FLUIDS} format) and is resolved to Forge fluids
282: * exactly once here (the pool keeps the abstract form). Same three-tier contract as {@link #deliver}:
283: * <ol>
284: * <li><strong>Output hatches first</strong> (an ME output hatch is the bulk sink: it takes the full amount into
285: * its cache and flushes to the ME network) — void protection ON, the accepted mB comes back,</li>
286: * <li><strong>The fluid pool</strong> for whatever the hatches could not take (buffered until the pump-out
287: * finds room),</li>
288: * <li>Overflow that the pool could not hold is logged LOUDLY (a fluid cannot be dropped as an entity — this is
289: * the one loud-loss escape hatch, and it requires a full 160k L pool to overflow).</li>
290: * </ol>
291: *
292: * @param entries abstract fluid entry list ({@code USSShipCargo.TAG_FLUIDS} format)
293: * @return true if everything was accepted (no loss), false if some was lost (and logged)

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\multiblock\MultiblockAudit.java ====
11: * Pure audit of the multiblock component cells inside an assembler's scan volume (no world/MTE references —
12: * testable in a bare JVM).
13: *
14: * <p>
15: * Rules (per user spec):
16: *
17: * <ul>
18: * <li>a component controller present in the volume whose own structure is NOT formed →
19: * {@link #ERROR_INCOMPLETE} (the component has not been built as a multiblock — its stats do not apply).</li>
20: * <li>a component whose controllers are ALL formed but whose cells in the volume are fewer than
21: * {@code expectedCells × controllers} → {@link #ERROR_OUT_OF_VOLUME} (part of the structure sticks out of the
22: * volume — digitizing would capture a broken component).</li>
23: * <li>stray / extra casings (including casings without any controller) → no error: tolerated as inert mass (the
24: * player can use them for decoration); they simply carry their small mass into the digitized stats.</li>
25: * </ul>
29: * Structure of a found component is not formed.
32: * A formed component's structure extends beyond the scanned volume.
38: * One audited component type: its registration data plus the cell counts of a single scan.
61: * @param audits one {@link ComponentAudit} per component type found in the volume
62: * @return the error keys (empty when the volume is fine); each key appears at most once
68: no controllers of this type in the volume: stray casings are tolerated (inert mass)

13: * Per-assembler client visuals: an immutable state snapshot published by the assemblers from their
14: * {@code onPostTick} (server thread) and consumed by {@link RenderVoidcraftAssembler} (render thread).

16: *
17: * <p>

19: * {@code WorldServer} while the render thread reads the {@code WorldClient} — two different objects for the
20: * same dimension — so identity checks against the rendered world would always skip the snapshot. The render
21: * handler skips snapshots from other dimensions.
22: *
23: * <p>

25: * {@link #previewOffset}) are the single source of truth for the scan volume's placement — they mirror the
26: * assemblers' own scan basis exactly (the machine's front face spanned by two perpendicular axes, the
27: * build volume extending in front of it).
31: * Both assemblers are 3 blocks deep (the machine body behind the controller).

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\render\RenderVoidcraftAssembler.java ====
41: * World-last renderer for the two assemblers (voidcraft + voidbase):
42: * <ul>
43: * <li>while the machine's own structure is valid — a transparent, pulsing cyan wireframe around the scan
44: * area;</li>
45: * <li>while digitizing — two transparent pulsing cyan planes sweeping from both ends of the volume towards
46: * the middle;</li>
47: * <li>whenever the volume holds components — a rotating 1/4-scale cyan hologram of the current build
48: * behind the machine, even while the machine is idle or the build is invalid (the building-progress
49: * preview). Its location is set by {@link #PREVIEW_DISTANCE} / {@link #PREVIEW_HEIGHT}.</li>
50: * </ul>

52: * <p>

54: * all opaque geometry (world, planets, hulls, EoH space shell) has rendered and written depth — so nothing
55: * can overpaint the overlay; the overlay depth-tests against it (LEQUAL) but never writes depth, so it is
56: * see-through and never occludes the world. All draws are shader-based and camera-relative: the camera-
57: * interpolated translation is folded into each model matrix (the handlers' state block sets up blend /
58: * cull / depth / alpha-test, the shaders are unlit and unfogged).
63: * Preview hologram scale: 1/4 of the real build size.

81: * Full period of the scanning-plane sweep in ticks (ends → middle → ends).
84: * Overlay cyan (the fleet beams' cyan).
87: * Wireframe alpha at full pulse.
90: * Scanning-plane alpha at full pulse.
93: * Preview hologram tint and opacity (the voidbase blueprint hologram's look).
99: * Per-assembler preview state (render thread only).
102: * Render frame the build volume was last re-scanned (0 = not scanned yet).

117: * shader are rebaked and their attribute locations may have moved).

140: is folded into every model matrix below (the shaders draw camera-relative geometry).

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\render\RenderVoidcraftBlueprintItem.java ====
27: * Draws the blueprint items (the digitized Voidcraft and the Voidbase blueprint) as the ACTUAL 3D model of the
28: * craft they carry — using the same VAO the in-flight fleet renderer draws (see {@link ShipModelBuilder} and
29: * {@link VoidcraftShipModelCache}) — in inventory slots, in hand, and when dropped, instead of the single flat
30: * texture. The Voidbase blueprint is drawn as a half-transparent cyan hologram; the Voidcraft is drawn solid.
31: *
32: * <p>
33: * Registered per item via
34: * {@link net.minecraftforge.client.MinecraftForgeClient#registerItemRenderer} (see
35: * {@code ClientProxy#registerRenderInfo}). Forge places the model inside the standard 3D item-box transform it
36: * applies for block-style item icons, so in slot/hand the model fills the item box ([0,1]³ centered at
37: * (0.5, 0.5, 0.5)), scaled so the longest blueprint axis fits the slot; a dropped item is centered at the
38: * entity origin instead.
39: *
40: * <p>
41: * Stacks without a blueprint payload (empty or corrupt) are not claimed, so the vanilla flat icon renders for
42: * them.
47: * Fraction of the item box the model is scaled to (leaves a small margin inside the slot).
50: * Scratch for the model-matrix composition (client render thread only).
54: * The Voidbase blueprint model is drawn as a half-transparent cyan hologram: the tint color and opacity below,
55: * alpha-blended, with depth writes off so the shell is see-through in both directions.
62: * Whether this renderer draws the Voidbase blueprint (station) item or the Voidcraft (ship) item.
70: * @param maxAxis the longest blueprint axis in cells

72: *         a degenerate model)
79: * @param stack the item stack
80: * @return true if the stack carries a blueprint grid payload
127: Fill the item box (centered at (0.5, 0.5, 0.5) in the transform the caller set up) — or, for a
128: dropped item, center the model on the entity origin, which the caller already placed.
134: The blueprint cells span 0..n-1 on each axis — center them on the origin.
137: Culling off for the model (the hull is a hollow shell of blocks plus thin cover quads — the back
138: sides are depth-occluded by the cube volume, so disabling culling only guarantees the faces we want
139: actually draw).
147: The Voidbase blueprint is drawn as a half-transparent cyan hologram: alpha-blended, alpha test
148: lowered so the 0.5-alpha fragments survive, depth writes off so the shell is see-through in

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\render\RenderVoidcraftShip.java ====
47: * Renders the ship fleet hologram (Phase 4 pass 5): the USS's WHOLE in-flight fleet (dozens–hundreds of ships, see
48: * {@link USSConstants#MAX_SHIPS_PER_USS}) as face-culled block VBOs (see {@link ShipModelBuilder}) animated around
49: * the Unstable Solar System — one invisible fleet anchor block carries the entry list (payload + state +
50: * gateway/hover rel per ship), and this renderer draws every entry.
51: *
52: * <p>
53: * Animation is fully client-side: the server writes each entry's state on every transition, and the client
54: * advances a local progress value per leg using the same {@link USSConstants#legTicks} durations the server ticks
55: * with (both read the ship speed / mining power from the payload NBT), so no per-tick sync is needed.
56: *
57: * <ul>
58: * <li>DOCKED — hovers at the anchor (near the star)</li>
59: * <li>OUTBOUND — gateway → hover point (the per-mission target's live position, inside the space shell)</li>
60: * <li>MINING — hovers 0.5 blocks above the target planet (Starlifters 2.5 above the star), nose forward (keeps
61: * the arrival heading — it does not turn to face the body), tracking its rendered position</li>
62: * <li>RETURNING — hover point → gateway</li>
63: * </ul>
64: *
65: * <p>
66: * <strong>Dynamic destinations (pass 7):</strong> each entry carries a mission TARGET (a planet index or
67: * {@code -1} for the star, chosen by the USS at launch), and the fleet TE carries the system's planet specs +
68: * star size. The hover point is resolved client-side every frame — the planet's LIVE rendered position (the exact
69: * orbit math of {@code EOHRenderingUtils.renderUSSOrbits}, see {@link USSFleetOrbit#planetAnchorPosition}) — so
70: * the ship hovers precisely above the planet it sees, and the OUTBOUND/RETURNING legs continuously re-aim at
71: * where the planet IS, not where it was at launch.
72: *
73: * <p>
74: * Swarm spread: with a large fleet every ship would otherwise stack at its target's hover point. Each ship
75: * instead hovers at a stable per-ship offset around that point ({@link USSFleetOrbit#offsetFor}) — deterministic,
76: * shell-bounded, computed client-side from the per-launch SEED the USS assigned (unique per flight even for
77: * duplicated ship items; the item UUID is only the legacy fallback).
78: *
79: * <p>
80: * Orientation: the nose (the ship's visual front — the blueprint's FAR end, grid +Z, the end built AWAY from the
81: * assembler; pass 24 flip) follows the
82: * flight: direction of travel on OUTBOUND/RETURNING, and the ship keeps that ARRIVAL heading while MINING (pass
83: * 10: it does not rotate to face the body being worked), unrotated while DOCKED. Heading changes are eased per
84: * ship (keyed by the per-launch seed), so the flip on the RETURNING leg reads as a deliberate turn (there is no
85: * constant display spin).
86: *
87: * <p>
88: * <strong>Pass 8 effects:</strong> while a MINER or STARLIFTER is in its MINING leg, a thin additive laser rod
89: * runs from the ship's middle to the body's middle (fading over the leg's ends — see {@link VoidcraftShipFx});
90: * while a ship moves (OUTBOUND / RETURNING), smoke exhaust is emitted behind it, the opposite of its travel
91: * direction. The Constructor is the only role that does not fire the beam (it builds, per user spec).
97: * Hologram scale: each ship cell renders at this fraction of a normal block — the ships are distant specks on
98: * their flight path (user request: "very small" — 1/16 originally, pass 10: even smaller, 1/48 = 3× smaller).
103: * Heading ease time constant (ticks): the ship turns toward its target heading with an exponential approach
104: * ({@code 1 - e^(-dt/τ)}), so a heading change (e.g. the flip between the outbound and returning legs) reads
105: * as a deliberate maneuver over ~2 seconds instead of an instant snap.
109: * Scratch model matrix (the render thread is single; one instance serves every per-draw upload).
113: * Cached per-gateway geometry (keyed by the gateway's anchor-relative block coords): the event-plane disc +
114: * the opaque tube, baked ONCE in ANCHOR-LOCAL coordinates and positioned per frame by the model matrix
115: * (translation to the anchor block center) — the same cached-VAO pattern as the ship hulls.
130: * Clears the cached gateway geometry (also called on resource reload, where VAOs are deleted outright).
139: * Per-ship animation phase (last seen state + the tick it was first seen, plus the eased heading).

163: FIX (flight desync): a travel leg lerps from a FIXED start point (the ship's position at the leg's
164: FIRST frame) to its destination, with cumulative progress as t. Using the LIVE lastPos (updated every
165: frame) as the lerp start made it self-referential — pos_N = lerp(pos_{N-1}, dest, progress_N) with
166: CUMULATIVE progress — which converged the ship to the destination in a few seconds while the counter
167: ran the full leg. The start is captured once (see renderShip) and held for the whole leg.

287: * The revealed spacetime ripples (the Explorer pass): each is a camera-facing (billboard) equilateral TRIANGLE in
288: * a pulsating dark blue, semi-transparent — the "spacetime ripple" reading. The pulse (size + alpha) is a function
289: * of world time, so every client animates it identically without any per-tick sync.
290: *
291: * <p>
292: * Drawn through the ripple shader ({@link VoidcraftShaders#ripple()}): the triangle is billboarded in the
293: * vertex stage with the camera's right/up axes read from the model-view matrix (so it always faces the
294: * player, regardless of where it sits on a shell). Culling is off (billboard winding), blend is standard
295: * alpha (transparent, NOT additive glow), and depth writes are off (a pure overlay, still depth-TESTED so
296: * opaque geometry correctly occludes it).
297: *
298: * @param ripples   the revealed ripple positions — each {@code [x, y, z]} in fleet-anchor blocks (never null)
299: * @param x,y,z     the anchor block CENTER in camera-relative coordinates (the ripple positions are added to it)
300: * @param worldTime the world's total tick count (drives the pulse)
311: standard alpha (transparent)
314: Camera-facing basis (billboard): the camera's RIGHT and UP axes in world space are the first two
315: ROWS of the model-view matrix's rotation part — (m00,m01,m02) = (buf[0],buf[4],buf[8]) and
316: (m10,m11,m12) = (buf[1],buf[5],buf[9]). (The first two COLUMNS are the images of the world X/Y
317: axes, correct only for an unrotated camera — that made the triangles tilt with the world instead
318: of tracking the camera.)
320: The FLOAT matrix overload + org.lwjgl.BufferUtils (the FrameMatrices pattern) — the double variant
321: (glGetDouble into a java.nio.DoubleBuffer) is rejected by this LWJGL build ("DoubleBuffer is not
322: direct"). The read is additionally guarded: the effect degrades to a fixed world-up orientation
323: rather than crashing the game over a GL/environment quirk.
330: m00 → right.x
331: m01 → right.y
332: m02 → right.z
333: m10 → up.x
334: m11 → up.y
335: m12 → up.z

501: points at the star. Degenerate (gateway at the star center): any axis is equally valid.
514: Anchor-LOCAL gate center (the anchor-block translation is applied per frame by the model matrix).
523: An orthonormal basis (u, v) in the gate plane (perpendicular to the axis n), built with two cross
524: products from a helper axis that is not parallel to n (world up, or world X when n ≈ ±Y).
530: u = a × n
535: v = n × u
537: 1) The event plane: a flat disc at the tube center — a triangle fan around the center.
548: 2) The tube: SEGS quads per ring (outer wall, bore wall) + SEGS quads per end cap annulus.
551: a) the OUTER WALL (radius RO): a ring between the two end faces.
562: b) the BORE WALL (radius RI): the inner surface of the tube.
573: c) the END CAPS: the annulus at each end face (a ring between the bore and outer circles).

872: The model matrix: translate to the ship position, scale to hologram cell size, orient, and center (cells
873: span 0..n-1 on each axis). Orientation: headingFor returns (yaw, pitch) in DEGREES; JOML's rotate()
874: takes RADIANS (the codebase-wide convention — every other JOML caller wraps its degrees in
875: Math.toRadians). The derivation applies YAW to the model first, then PITCH, i.e. the model rotation is
876: R_pitch * R_yaw (yaw on the right, applied to the vertex first). JOML post-multiplies each rotate()
877: call, so PITCH must be issued first and YAW second (yaw-then-pitch yields R_yaw * R_pitch, which points
878: the nose off-target for any diagonal direction).
886: Culling off for the ship: the hull is a hollow shell of blocks plus thin cover quads, and we cannot
887: assume every face's winding from the outside — the back sides are depth-occluded by the cube volume,
888: so disabling culling only guarantees the faces we want (covers included) actually draw. Blending off:
889: the hull is opaque (every block atlas pixel is fully opaque), and blending would inherit whatever
890: blend FUNC an earlier tile-entity renderer in the same pass left active (an ambient additive
891: (SRC_ALPHA, ONE) makes an alpha-1.0 fragment read as "slightly transparent": dst = src + dst, the
892: star layer behind bleeds through — visible from the camera angles that look at the ship against the
893: star). Blend OFF is immune to the function and matches the legacy unblended hull look.

1038: * The laser queue: the beam endpoints (mining + construction lasers) are captured here during the tile-entity
1039: * pass and drawn once per frame in the {@code RenderWorldLastEvent} pass ({@link BeamWorldLastRenderer}) — the
1040: * one moment when all opaque geometry (the world, the planets, the ships and bases, the EoH space shell) has
1041: * already rendered and written its depth. A beam drawn there composites over the starfield in any tile-entity
1042: * draw order (a beam drawn earlier in the frame is overpainted by a shell that renders later), and, writing no
1043: * depth of its own, leaves nothing behind for the shell to fail its depth test against (a depth-writing beam
1044: * would leave an unpainted window in the shell). Each entry: start xyz, end xyz, fade, worldTime, r, g, b.
1056: * Draws the queued lasers ({@link #BEAM_QUEUE}) in the {@code RenderWorldLastEvent} pass, once per frame. The
1057: * event fires with the world's modelview still active and the captured endpoints are in the same
1058: * camera-relative frame the tile-entity pass used, so the rod shader draws them with the identity model
1059: * matrix.
1060: *
1061: * <p>
1062: * Each beam is the shared rod VAO ({@link VoidcraftGeometry#beamRod()}), positioned entirely by the beam
1063: * shader (endpoints + cross-section axes + half-width), in a bright cyan with additive blending and a gentle
1064: * pulse — the classic "laser" reading. Culling is off (the rod's winding must not fight the world's
1065: * GL_CULL_FACE) and depth writes are off — the beams are drawn in the world-last pass, after every opaque
1066: * geometry has written its depth, so the depth test alone gives the correct picture (the planet's surface
1067: * and the hull occlude the part of the rod behind them, the starfield shows behind it) and no later opaque
1068: * draw can overpaint it.

1104: endpoints coincide — degenerate geometry, nothing to draw
1138: * One full size-BREATH cycle, in ticks — ~3 s per pulse (a slow, gentle breathing, not a strobe).
1140: * Breathing AMPLITUDE: the half-size oscillates ±10% about the base.
1142: * One full SPIN about the world up (Y) axis, in ticks — ~24 s per revolution (a slow, steady turn).
1147: * The Explorer's scanning effect: a translucent, additive-glow cube around the ship while it scans — an
1148: * energy halo that glows against any background (space or planet surface).
1149: *
1150: * <p>
1151: * The field is a LIVING halo: (a) it gently BREATHES — its size oscillates ±10% on a ~3-second sine
1152: * ({@link #SCAN_PULSE_PERIOD_TICKS} / {@link #SCAN_PULSE_AMPLITUDE}); (b) it SPINS slowly about the world
1153: * up (Y) axis (one revolution per {@link #SCAN_SPIN_PERIOD_TICKS}) in a static 45°-on-all-three-axes pose —
1154: * the cube's corner points up, reading as a diamond; the spin is issued first (the world-space transform)
1155: * so the whole diamond turns about the vertical axis. The fractional render time drives (a) and (b), so
1156: * they animate smoothly frame-to-frame.
1157: *
1158: * <p>
1159: * Drawn through the flat-color shader ({@link VoidcraftShaders#color()}) with the unit cube: cull OFF
1160: * (winding-independent), ADDITIVE blend (the glow pattern of the beams), depth WRITES off (a pure
1161: * overlay; it is still depth-TESTED, so the dome/shell occlusion stays correct).
1162: *
1163: * @param center     the cube center in the anchor frame (the ship's current position)
1164: * @param renderTime fractional render time in TICKS ({@code worldTime + partialTicks}) — drives the size
1165: *                   pulse and the spin about the up axis
1166: * @param half       the cube half-size in blocks
1167: * @param seed       the per-launch seed (unused — the field is a pure function of renderTime)
1175: additive — the scan field GLOWS, visible against any
1176: background
1179: The pose: spin about the world UP (Y) axis (issued first — the world-space transform, so the whole
1180: diamond turns about the vertical axis), then the static 45°-on-all-three-axes pose (the gem shape).
1181: JOML's rotate() takes RADIANS (spinDeg is degrees).
1183: The size: a smooth ±10% breathing driven by the fractional render time (one cycle per

1185: keeps the sine argument small even for huge world times.)
1211: * Local leg progress in [0, 1], derived from the same leg duration the server ticks with.

1225: *                       instead of

1243: picks the table) — the client must match the server's duration or the hover/leg progress drifts.

1279: * blueprint's FAR end, away from the assembler — a player builds the ship pointing away from the machine), so
1280: * the rotation maps the model +Z axis straight onto d. Applying yaw (around +Y) then pitch (around +X) maps
1281: * +Z onto (dx,dy,dz) exactly via: yaw = atan2(dx, sqrt(dy²+dz²)), pitch = −atan2(dy, dz) (derivation:
1282: * R_x(φ)·R_y(θ)·ẑ = (sinθ, −cosθ·sinφ, cosθ·cosφ); the pass-10 asin form was only exact for level or vertical
1283: * flight).

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\render\ShipModelBuilder.java ====
24: * Builds the ship hologram VAO from a {@link VoidcraftBlueprint} (client only).
25: *
26: * <p>
27: * Every occupied blueprint cell is rendered with the vanilla
28: * {@link net.minecraft.client.renderer.RenderBlocks#renderBlockAsItem} pipeline — real block textures, real
29: * per-face UVs, ambient occlusion disabled — into a single captured {@link IVertexArrayObject} in the shared
30: * textured shader's vertex format (position + UV), one draw call at render time. Covers mounted on a visible
31: * face are added as thin textured quads just outside that face (the cover's own icon from the block atlas).
32: *
33: * <p>
34: * Face culling is precomputed from the grid itself: a face touching an occupied neighbor cell is hidden
35: * (all ship components are opaque machine blocks), and fully enclosed cells are skipped entirely.
36: *
37: * <p>
38: * Must be called on the client main thread (texture binding + tessellator capture).
44: * Component MTE ids start here (MetaTileEntityIDs 32058–32066, one per {@link VoidcraftComponent} in meta
45: * order). All component blocks live on the single GT machine block.
54: * Build the hologram for this blueprint.
55: *
56: * @param blueprint the digitized ship (w/h/d + component grid)
57: * @return the built model (never null — an empty grid yields an empty model, which renders nothing)
64: Occupancy (grid value 0 = empty).
74: Per-cell face visibility (neighbor occupied → hide the shared face).
111: Pass 1: the hull blocks (real machine textures), in the renderer's default GL state.
128: Pass 2: the covers — one textured quad per mounted cover on a visible face. The quads are wound
129: CCW-from-outside (matching the block faces) and float 0.003 outside the hull, so they render
130: correctly under whatever culling/depth state the ship is drawn with.
151: * The vertex format the ship model is captured into: the shared textured shader's format (position + UV,
152: * in that shader's attribute order). The direct tessellator writes only the elements present in the format
153: * (a normal for the hull faces is dropped), and the model is drawn with that shader.
160: The shaders are not (re)loaded yet — capture nothing meaningful; the capture stays a no-op empty VAO
161: and the next resource reload re-bakes the shader and clears the model cache.
166: * Draw a cover quad on every VISIBLE face of the cell that has a mounted cover. The quad sits just outside
167: * the hull face (0.003 offset — no z-fight) and is textured with the cover's own icon, so the in-flight
168: * ship shows exactly what the in-world face shows.
180: no cover, or buried in the hull (not an exposed face)
185: corrupt grid value
187: the icon lives in the block atlas (GTCustomBlockIconContainer) — read the live IIcon by its
188: registered name (avoids the BlockIcons dedup-map, which cleanup() clears)
191: icon never registered — skip rather than NPE
198: * One cover quad on the unit cube's {@code side} face (the caller has already translated to the cell), UVs
199: * from the cover icon, upright when the face is viewed from outside (side faces: world +Y up; top/bottom:
200: * arbitrary orientation).
203: unit-cube corners of the face, outward, in draw order — (x, y, z)
207: -Y
211: +Y
215: -Z
219: +Z
223: -X
227: +X
232: float the quad just outside the hull face

18: * The ship fleet anchor (Phase 4 pass 5): ONE invisible, non-collidable block per Unstable Solar System carrying
19: * the WHOLE in-flight fleet (up to {@code USSConstants.MAX_SHIPS_PER_USS} ships) as a list of entries, each with
20: * the ship payload + state + mission target + anchor-relative gateway the client renderer needs — plus the
21: * system's planet specs + star size the client resolves the targets against (pass 7).
22: *
23: * <p>
24: * Pass 4 stored one ship per block (a block per slot); pass 5 scales to dozens–hundreds of ships with a single
25: * fleet block — the cost of a 100-ship fleet is 100 small draw calls, not 100 world blocks / NBT blobs.
26: *
27: * <p>
28: * Pattern mirrors the legacy EoH render block (description packet + {@code onDataPacket} sync), but this is a
29: * brand-new TE — the legacy render classes are untouched.
30: *
31: * <p>
32: * Client animation (OUTBOUND/MINING/RETURNING) is driven locally from each entry's {@code state} plus the ship's
33: * speed/mining power (both inside the payload NBT) using the same {@code USSConstants} leg durations the server
34: * ticks with — the server only has to update the entry state on each transition (and each ship's animation phase +
35: * swarm spread are keyed client-side on the per-launch seed — see {@code tectech.voidcraft.uss.USSFleetOrbit} and
36: * {@link #TAG_ENTRY_SEED}).
37: *
38: * <p>
39: * Pass 7: the TE also carries the SYSTEM (planet specs + star size, {@link #setSystem}) — the client resolves
40: * each entry's mission target ({@link #TAG_ENTRY_TARGET}) against it to hover above the planet's live rendered
41: * position (or 2.5 blocks above the star center for Starlifters).
47: * Entry tags — written by the USS MTE, read by the client renderer.
53: * Entry tag (pass 7): the ship's mission target — a planet index into {@link #getSystemPlanets()} (the ship
54: * hovers 0.5 blocks above that planet's rendered position), a RIPPLE-POINT index (0..342, the Explorer pass)
55: * for Explorers, or {@code -1} for the star itself (Starlifters hover 2.5 above the star center). The
56: * destination is resolved CLIENT-side against the system below — the pass-5 static role hover point
57: * ({@code vc_orbit_rel}) is gone.
62: * Entry tag (the Explorer pass): the ship's RESOLVED destination in fleet-anchor blocks (the server resolved it
63: * at launch — the planet shell point, the star center, or the ripple point's position). Present when the ship
64: * has a destination; the client uses it directly (the ripple point index in {@link #TAG_ENTRY_TARGET} is NOT a
65: * planet index, so {@code targetBody} cannot resolve it). Absent for legacy entries → the client falls back to
66: * {@code targetBody(target)}.
71: * Entry tag (pass 26 — the travel-time rendering fix): the ship's TRAVEL DISTANCE in fleet-anchor blocks
72: * (the server's {@code VoidcraftActiveShip.travelDistance}, the block-separation between the launch origin and
73: * the destination). Written by the USS MTE, read by the client's leg-progress math. The client USED to read
74: * {@code vc_tdist} off the ship PAYLOAD, where it was never written — so every travel leg animated at the
75: * minimum floor (the ship "zipped" across the system in 1 s regardless of the server's real minutes-long
76: * duration, and the working-state beam/scan never had a visible window). Now the ACTUAL distance rides along
77: * in the entry and the client animates each leg for its true length.
82: * System tags (pass 7): the planet specs + star size the client renders the planets AND the ships' dynamic
83: * hover targets from (the same specs the star render TE draws — self-contained, no world lookups).

90: * been scanned (revealed) and IS a ripple. Each entry is {@code [x, y, z]} in fleet-anchor blocks (the same
91: * coordinate frame the ships + star use). The client renders each as a pulsating dark-blue transparent triangle.

98: * gateway machine that is locked onto this USS (the MTE registers them on its launch-target scan). The client

100: * the system view, so they ride with the system data and render even when no ship is in flight.

107: * item-UUID fallback).

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\render\VoidcraftGeometry.java ====
18: * The static unit geometry shared by the voidcraft effect shaders. Everything is unit-sized (corners at ±1, or
19: * radius 1) so a single VAO serves every draw: the per-instance position / size / orientation is the model
20: * matrix the draw uploads.
21: *
22: * <ul>
23: * <li>{@link #unitCube()} — the six faces of the unit cube as triangles: the Explorer scan cube, the Voidbase
24: * site fill box, the gateway event plane and tube are all unit geometry scaled by a model matrix.</li>
25: * <li>{@link #unitCubeLines()} — the unit cube's 12 edges as GL_LINES: the Voidbase site wireframes and the
26: * assembler wireframes.</li>
27: * <li>{@link #unitQuad()} — one quad (z = 0, x/y ∈ [-1, 1]): the assembler scan planes.</li>
28: * <li>{@link #rippleTriangle()} — the unit equilateral triangle (circumradius 1, apex up, z = 0): the Explorer
29: * ripple billboards.</li>
30: * <li>{@link #beamRod()} — the laser rod cross-section (four side quads, t ∈ [0, 1] along the axis): every
31: * beam; endpoints / axes / half-width come from the beam shader's uniforms.</li>
32: *
33: * <p>
34: * Each VAO is built lazily from the vertex format of its owning shader and is deleted by {@link #release()}
35: * (resource reload) — the attribute locations may move on a re-bake.
47: * The unit cube (corners at ±1 on every axis), six faces as triangles.
57: * The unit cube's 12 edges as GL_LINES.
68: * One unit quad (x/y ∈ [-1, 1] at z = 0).
81: * The unit equilateral triangle (circumradius 1, apex up) at z = 0.
94: * The laser rod: four side quads spanning t ∈ [0, 1] (the start → end parameter) and the (±1, ±1)
95: * cross-section corners.

11: * The voidcraft effect shaders (the 3.3 core-profile-first convention with the 2.1 fallback):
12: *
13: * <ul>

16: * plane and tube.</li>

18: * cross-section axes + half-width as uniforms) so one static VAO serves every beam.</li>

20: * as uniforms).</li>

24: * may change attribute locations, so every captured / cached VAO built against these (or the shared textured)
25: * formats is discarded and rebuilt lazily on the next draw.

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\render\VoidcraftShipFx.java ====
9: * Pass 8 — pure math for the client-side ship effects (mining laser beam + exhaust), kept out of the GL class so
10: * it unit-tests in a bare JVM (no LWJGL, no {@code Minecraft} statics).
11: *
12: * <p>
13: * <strong>Beam</strong> (user spec): during the MINING leg, a thin laser rod from the MIDDLE of the ship to the
14: * MIDDLE of the body it works — for MINERS and STARLIFTERS (a Constructor builds, it does not fire). The rod
15: * fades in over the first {@link #BEAM_FADE_RAMP} of the leg and out over the last, so the state transitions
16: * (OUTBOUND→MINING→RETURNING) read as the beam engaging and releasing instead of popping.
17: *
18: * <p>
19: * <strong>Exhaust</strong> (user spec): particles emitted BEHIND the ship — the opposite of its direction of
20: * travel — on the legs it actually moves (OUTBOUND / RETURNING), not while it hovers.
25: * Beam half-width in blocks — a thin laser rod that is still visible across the system. Pass 10 (user: "quite
26: * wide — ~4× smaller is fine, very narrow is fine"): a quarter of the original 0.045 → 0.01125. Pass 26 (user:
27: * "the mining beams are no longer rendering for miners that are mining — miners orbiting a planet don't have
28: * any beam on them"): the 0.01125 rod was ~2 cm wide and, for a body-miner, mostly occluded inside the body
29: * (only a short stub above the surface was visible) — so it effectively disappeared. Widened to a clearly
30: * visible ~8 cm rod (0.04) so the mining laser reads again. Pass 28 (user: "the mining laser beam was
31: * previously made thicker, but it was good previously — make it 4 times less thick"): a quarter of 0.04 →
32: * 0.01 (~2 cm rod, close to the pass-10 width the user liked).
37: * Beam fade ramp as a FRACTION of the mining leg: fully on between the first and last {@value} of the leg.
38: * Pass 26: shortened from 0.15 to 0.08 so the beam is at full strength for a wider middle of the leg (the
39: * engage/release fade is kept, just gentler — the beam is visible for longer instead of a narrow window).
46: * Whether the mining laser fires for the given ship (user spec: MINER and STARLIFTER — a Constructor builds
47: * during its leg and does not fire; a pure transport has no mining leg at all).
48: *
49: * @param payload the ship payload NBT (carries {@link VoidcraftNbt#TAG_ROLES}); null-safe
60: * Beam visibility for the mining-leg progress in [0, 1]: 0 at either end, 1 in the middle, with a linear ramp
61: * of {@link #BEAM_FADE_RAMP} at both ends (symmetric: {@code beamFade(p) == beamFade(1 - p)}).
72: * Beam basis for the rod between two (world) endpoints: the normalized direction {@code d} plus two
73: * orthonormal perpendiculars {@code p1}/{@code p2} (a right-handed frame, {@code p1 x p2 = d}).
74: *
75: * @param start the ship center (world coordinates)
76: * @param end   the body center (world coordinates)
77: * @return {@code {dx, dy, dz, p1x, p1y, p1z, p2x, p2y, p2z}}, or null when the endpoints coincide
78: *         (degenerate geometry — the renderer then draws nothing)
91: Reference vector NOT parallel to d (d is usually vertical — a straight-down mining pose — so prefer the
92: X axis in that case; the Y axis only when d is far from vertical).
100: p1 = normalize(d x r); p2 = d x p1 (unit by construction, right-handed).
115: * Exhaust spawn gate: deterministic (no RNG state on the render thread) — fires on exactly 3 of every 8
116: * consecutive world ticks, offset by the ship's per-launch seed so a fleet does not puff in lockstep.
117: *

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\ship\VoidcraftBlueprint.java ====
9: * A Voidcraft blueprint: the placed component grid (with per-cell facing), the per-face cover grid, and the stats
10: * and role derived from them.
11: *
12: * <p>
13: * Grid encodings (all {@code index = x + width * (y + height * z)}):
14: *
15: * <ul>
16: * <li>{@link #grid}: 0 = empty cell, otherwise {@link VoidcraftComponent#toGridValue()} (1..9).</li>
17: * <li>{@link #facingGrid}: 0 = empty cell, otherwise {@code ForgeDirection ordinal + 1} (1..6) — the facing the
18: * component block was placed with. A cell without facing data is treated as facing DOWN (the MTE default facing).</li>
19: * <li>{@link #coverGrid}: length {@code cells * 6}, {@code index = cell * 6 + side}, 0 = no cover, otherwise
20: * {@link VoidcraftCoverComponent#toGridValue()} (1..8).</li>
21: * </ul>
22: *
23: * <p>
24: * This is the exact payload stored inside an {@code ItemVoidcraft} (see {@link VoidcraftNbt}).
25: *
26: * <p>
27: * <b>Component model (pass 23).</b> Covers are the primary components: the ONLY placeable full blocks are the
28: * {@link VoidcraftComponent#CONTROLLER} and the {@link VoidcraftComponent#FRAME} (Voidcraft Frame); every other
29: * catalog entry is cover-only, and a cell holding one fails validation
30: * ({@code voidcraft_cover_only_component}). A ship also needs at least one frame hull block
31: * ({@code voidcraft_no_frame}) — the block whose faces accept the covers that carry all ship functionality.
32: *
33: * <p>
34: * <b>Thrust model (pass 18/19, pass 23, pass 24 flip).</b> Thrust is a <em>single value</em>, not directional.
35: * The assembler scans the volume in FRONT of its machine, so grid +Z is the FAR end (away from the assembler) and
36: * grid −Z is the assembler side. A player builds the ship pointing away from the machine, so the FAR end (grid +Z)
37: * is the ship's NOSE — in flight the ship travels away from where it was built and the cockpit leads (see
38: * {@code RenderVoidcraftShip.headingFor}). The ship's BACK is the assembler side (grid −Z, {@link #BACK_FACE}).
39: * Thrust comes ONLY from {@link VoidcraftCoverComponent#THRUSTER_NOZZLE} covers mounted on that back face (exhaust
40: * out the rear). Thrust = the plain sum of those magnitudes.
41: *
42: * <p>
43: * (Pass 18/20 had this inverted — nose on the assembler side, nozzle on the far end — which playtested as "the
44: * nozzle has to face the front of the ship". Pass 24 flips the whole convention.)
45: *
46: * <p>
47: * A thruster that does NOT face the back fails validation ({@code voidcraft_thruster_wrong_facing}), and a
48: * back-facing thruster needs the {@link #EXHAUST_CLEARANCE} blocks directly on its exhaust side (grid −Z, toward
49: * the assembler) free of Voidcraft blocks — a Voidcraft block there (the hull between the nozzle and the open
50: * exhaust) blocks the thruster (zero thrust from it) and fails validation ({@code voidcraft_engine_blocked}).
51: * The assembler machine and air on that side are not Voidcraft blocks, so a nozzle on the near end is always clear.
52: * Both reasons surface in the assembler GUI.
53: *
54: * <p>
55: * This class is pure Java (no Minecraft types) so the stat math stays unit-testable outside a game world.
60: * The ship's BACK face, as a ForgeDirection ordinal: NORTH (ordinal 2, −Z — the assembler side).
61: *
62: * <p>
63: * Pass 24 (flipped): the assembler scans the volume in front of its machine, so grid +Z is the FAR end (away
64: * from the assembler) and grid −Z is the assembler side. A player builds the ship pointing away from the
65: * machine, so the FAR end (grid +Z) is the ship's NOSE — in flight the ship travels away from where it was
66: * built (see {@code RenderVoidcraftShip.headingFor}). The rear is the assembler side, and a thruster fires
67: * the ship forward when mounted on a cell's NORTH (−Z) face.
72: * Thruster clearance: a back-facing thruster fires out its BACK face (grid −Z, the assembler side). It needs
73: * the {@code EXHAUST_CLEARANCE} cells directly on that exhaust side (grid −Z, toward the assembler) free of
74: * Voidcraft blocks, otherwise the thruster is blocked and contributes nothing. The assembler machine and air
75: * out there are not Voidcraft blocks, so a nozzle on the near end (z=0) is always clear.
82: * Component grid, length == width * height * depth.
84: * Per-cell facing, length == grid.length (0 = empty cell, 1..6 = ForgeDirection ordinal + 1).
86: * Per-face covers, length == grid.length * 6 (0 = no cover, 1..8 = cover id + 1).

105: * @param grid   grid values (0 = empty, 1..9 = component); must be exactly width*height*depth long
106: * @throws IllegalArgumentException if dimensions are out of range or the grid length mismatches

433: * Pass 24: is the thruster at {@code cell} blocked?
434: *
435: * <p>
436: * The thruster fires out its BACK face (grid −Z, the assembler side — {@link #BACK_FACE}). The
437: * {@link #EXHAUST_CLEARANCE} cells directly on that EXHAUST side (grid −Z: z−1…z−5 from the thruster cell —
438: * toward the assembler) must be free of Voidcraft component blocks. "Empty" = no Voidcraft block; the assembler
439: * machine and any air out there are NOT Voidcraft blocks, so they never block the exhaust. A Voidcraft block in
440: * any of those 5 cells (i.e. the hull is between the nozzle and the open exhaust) blocks the thruster.
441: *

444: * are outside the hull (toward the assembler), so it contributes thrust with no clearance requirement.

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\ship\VoidcraftComponent.java ====
9: * The Voidcraft component catalog.
10: *
11: * <p>
12: * PASS 23 (user spec): <b>covers are the primary components â€” all ship functionality comes from the covers.</b>
13: * Only TWO components are placeable full blocks:
14: *
15: * <ul>
16: * <li>{@link #CONTROLLER} â€” the ship's brain (required, exactly one per ship).</li>
17: * <li>{@link #FRAME} â€” the "Voidcraft Frame": a mostly-transparent framebox hull block whose purpose is to
18: * accept the Voidcraft component covers on its faces (renamed from the old Utility Block).</li>
19: * </ul>
20: *
21: * <p>
22: * The other entries ({@link #ENGINE}, {@link #CARGO_BAY}, ...) are <em>cover-only</em>: they are no longer
23: * placeable blocks, they survive in the catalog purely as the function definitions behind their mirror covers
24: * (icon/texture mapping and the stat shapes the covers copy). They are marked {@code placeable=false} â€” the
25: * assembler refuses to digitize a build volume that still contains them ({@code voidcraft_cover_only_component}).
26: *
27: * <p>
28: * <b>Multiblock components</b> (user spec) are a second kind of placeable block, marked {@code multiblock=true}:
29: * a GT multiblock defined as a StructureLib structure (its own controller machine block plus zero-stat casing
30: * blocks). The assemblers digitize the whole structure as a unit when the controller's own structure check
31: * passes; the component's stats apply exactly once, through the controller entry's per-cell contribution (the
32: * casing entries contribute mass only).
33: *
34: * <p>
35: * Each entry contributes fixed stats (see the per-field javadoc). The {@code tier} gates which assembler circuit
36: * tier may digitize it (higher tech = more options, per the rework proposal). The grid encoding used in
37: * {@link VoidcraftBlueprint} is {@code meta + 1} (0 = empty cell).
42: * Required, exactly one per ship. The Voidcraft's "brain": without it the build is rejected. Contributes base
43: * mass and a little integrity; every function of the ship comes from its covers.
49: * COVER-ONLY: thrust is delivered by the {@link VoidcraftCoverComponent#THRUSTER_NOZZLE} cover.
50: * Kept as the function definition behind that cover (icon + stat shape); not a placeable block.
55: * The "Voidcraft Frame" (renamed from the old "Voidcraft Utility Block"): the mostly-transparent
56: * framebox hull block. Structural mass + integrity, no function of its own â€” its purpose is to accept
57: * the Voidcraft component covers on its faces; all ship functionality comes from those covers.
62: * COVER-ONLY: cargo is delivered by the {@link VoidcraftCoverComponent#CARGO_POD} cover. Kept as the
63: * function definition behind that cover (icon + stat shape); not a placeable block.
69: * COVER-ONLY: mining is delivered by the {@link VoidcraftCoverComponent#MINING_ARRAY} cover. Kept as the
70: * function definition behind that cover (icon + stat shape); not a placeable block.

==== E:\Programming\Java\GT5-Unofficial\src\main\java\tectech\voidcraft\ship\VoidcraftCoverComponent.java ====
6: * A single Voidcraft <i>cover</i> component — a compact part that mounts on any face of a Voidcraft component block
7: * (and, like a GT cover, replaces that face's texture) and contributes its own stats to the ship.
8: *
9: * <p>
10: * Covers are the dense alternative to full component blocks: every block has six faces, so one hull block can carry
11: * up to six parts. That lets far more complex voidcraft fit into a smaller footprint.
12: *
13: * <p>
14: * Thrust covers ({@link #THRUSTER_NOZZLE}) count toward the ship's single thrust value only when mounted on the
15: * ship's BACK face (−Z, the assembler side, {@link VoidcraftBlueprint#BACK_FACE}) — exhaust out the rear pushes
16: * the ship forward (the nose is the far end, grid +Z); a nozzle mounted anywhere else is dead weight (see
17: * {@link VoidcraftBlueprint#computeStats()}).
18: *
19: * <p>
20: * PASS 23 (user spec): covers are the PRIMARY components — ALL ship functionality (thrust, cargo, mining, scanning,
21: * construction, starlifting, energy) is delivered by covers mounted on the two placeable full blocks: the
22: * Voidcraft Controller and the Voidcraft Frame (the renamed Utility Block, a mostly-transparent framebox whose
23: * purpose is to accept exactly these covers). The old full-block engines/cargos/... are no longer placeable —
24: * they survive only as the cover definitions' mirror targets.
25: *
26: * <p>
27: * There is intentionally no controller cover: the ship's brain must be a full block.
28: *
29: * <p>
30: * Grid encoding in {@link VoidcraftBlueprint#coverGrid}: {@code id + 1} (0 = no cover on that face).
59: * The repair bay (Voidbase construction framework): the repair work command restores the station's integrity
60: * over time while drawing this cover's energy. A base without a repair bay cannot repair itself.
66: * The solar panel — the first energy-generating component: a flat energy generation rate per game tick into
67: * the station's energy buffer (star-independent in this version).
72: * All covers, in id order.
89: * Energy generated per game tick (the solar panel; 0 for everything else).
122: * Language key (tt.voidcraft.cover.*).
128: * The full component block this cover is the compact version of.
129: * Used for icon mapping (covers reuse the component's icon set) and for role/thrust semantics.
144: * Thrust magnitude (pass 18/24: counted toward the ship only when mounted on the back face, −Z = the assembler
145: * side; 0 for non-thrusters).
187: * Grid value stored in the blueprint cover grid: {@code id + 1} (0 = empty face).
192: * Recover the cover from a grid value (0 = empty face, out of range = invalid).
